1946 ( 1946 का 25 ) की धारा 6 के साथ पठित धारा 5 की 
उप -धारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रवत्त शक्तियो का प्रयोग करते हुए , केन्द्रीय सरकार , 
राजस्मान सरकार की महमति से , एतदद्वारा, राजस्थान राज्य के जिला 
अजमेर के प्राथमिक स्वास्थ्य केन्द्र भिनाई, पुलिस थाना भिनाई, में 5 
सितम्बर, 1978 को हुई माग्जीलरी नर्स मिडवाइफ कुमारी लिटीशिया 
हैमिलटन की मृत्यु के सम्बन्ध में भारतीय दण्ड संहिता, 1860 ( 1860 
का 45 ) की धारा 302 तथा 376 के अधीन पणनीय अपराधों तथा 
उक्त अपराधों के मम्बन्ध में या उनसे गम्बन्धित प्रयत्नों, दुष्प्रेरणास्रो 
पौर षडयंत्रो तथा उन्हीं तथ्या के कारण उत्पन्न हुई वैसी ही कार्यवाई 
के दौरान झिए गए अन्य किसी अपराध का अन्वेषण करने के लिए 
दिल्ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापना के सदस्पो की शक्तियो एव क्षेत्राधिकार 
का सम्पूर्ण राजस्भान राज्य में विस्तार करती है ।

कालपा० 4005 . - सर्वश्री ग्वालियर केबल्म एण्ड कन्रक्टर्स लि . , 
नई दिल्ली के नाम मे 80, 058 रुपए के लिए पायात लाइसेम मंण्या 
पी /एन/ 28 62595/ सी / एपम एक्स / 69/ 31 /78, दिनाक 29- 11- 1978 जारी 
किया गया था और ओ बाद में नीति पुस्तका 1977- 78 ( जिल्ब- 2 ) के 
खण्ड-1 भाग "ख " की कडिका 31 ( 2), 31( 3), 32 ( 2 ) पौर 38 
की शो के अनुसार पी०वी०सी० रेजिन का आयात करने के लिए 
पायात (नियंत्रण ) प्रादेश , 1955 की धारा की 5 ( 3 ) ( 1 ) के अंतर्गत 
मनोनीत विनिर्माता सर्वश्री मन्नत प्लासमा प्रा . लि ., बी - 96 वजीरपुर , 
इण्डस्ट्रियल एरिया, दिल्ली को 1- 2- 79 को हस्तारित कर दिया था । 


मनोनीप्त फर्म जो कि लाइसेंसधारी है, ने बताया है कि मूल साइसेंम 
की दोनों मुद्रा विनिमय नियंत्रण प्रप्ति प्रौर सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति किसी 
भी सीमा शुल्क प्राधिकारी के पास पंजीकृत कराए बिना और पूर्ण रूप से 
उपयोग में लाए बिना ही उनसे खो गई/ अस्थानस्थ हो गई है । उक्त विवरण 
के समर्थन में मनोनीत फर्म ने भायात-निर्यात क्रियाविधि 1979- 80 की 
हैंडबुक की कंरिका 334 में दी गई शो के अनुसार एक शपथ -पत्र 
दाखिल किया है । मैं संतुष्ट ह कि साइसेंस की दोनों मूल प्रप्तियो मुद्रा 


( संयुक्त मुख्य नियंत्रक प्रायात मियात फा कार्यालय ) 


प्रादेश 


बम्बई , 25 सितम्बर , 1979 


विषयः सर्वश्री स्यूनाडिज ट्यूब इन्जीनियस , 28, गाला इन्धस्ट्रिीयल एस्टेट, इम्पिग रोड, मुसद, बम्बई-80 को 35,142 रुपए के लिए जारी किए गए 

लाईसेंस संख्या 2728085, दिनांक 10- 2- 76 की सीमा मुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति को रत्व करने का मादेश । 


मा० मा0 4006. -- सर्वभी, स्यूनाडिज ट्यूब इन्जीनियसं, बम्बई-80 को ए 60-2 ( ए ) और ए 6 6- 2 ( ख ) के अनर्गत निर्यात के मद अनुभेय प्रकार के सोम - 
संस कार्बन स्टील ट्यूम्म और कोल्ड डाल के पायात के लिए 35,142 रुपए का उपर्युक्त लाईसेस प्रदत्त किया गया था । अब उम्लाने उपयुक्त माईसेंस की 
सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति की अनुलिपि प्रति के लिए हम प्राधार पर प्रावेदन किया है कि उपर्युक्त लाइमेस का भूम सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति सीमा शुल्क 
कार्यालय , बम्बई के पास पंजीकृत करने के पश्चात और पाशिक रूप से उपयोग में लाने के पाचात् खो गई/ अम्यानस्थ हो गई है । कुल धनराशि 
जिसके लिए लाईमेंस जारी किया गया था वह 35, 142 रुपए है और कुल धनराशि जिसके लिए शोम राशि को पूरा करने के लिए अनुलिपि प्रति की प्राध 
पमझता है बह 8, 312 रुपए है । 


इम तर्क के ममर्थन मे , आवेदर ने पंजीयक और महानगरीय मिलाधीण , बम्बई के मम्मन माग कागज पर विधिवत् माझ्याकिन एक शपथपत्र दाखिल किया है ।

[ PART II - SEC . 3 ( i ) ] 
मैं संतुष्ट है कि साईसेंस संख्या 2728085, दिनांक 10- 2- 76 की मुल सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति बो गई पषमा प्रस्थानस्म हो गई । पौर निधेश घेता 
है कि प्रायवक को उपर्युक्त लाईसेंम की अनुलिपि सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रनि जारी की जाए । मूल मीना शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रति 2728085 दिनांक 10- 2- 76 
रर की जाती है । 

विषयः- लाईसेंस संख्या 2728085, दिनांक 10- 2- 76 की सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन प्रनि की अनुलिपि प्रति जारी करना । 

पापको यह सूचित किया जाता है कि सर्वश्री ल्यूबा डिज ट्यूब इन्जीनियर्स, सम्मई- 80 को लाईसेंस सख्या-पी / यू 2728085, दिनांक 10- 2- 78 को 
ममुलिपि सीमा शुल्क प्रयोजन पनि जारी की गई है । यह अनुरोध किया जाता है कि मूल लाईसेंस (जिसका पीरा नीचे दिया गया है ) प्रस्तुत करने पर बैष 
म समझा जाए और यदि उपर्युक्त लाईसेंस की मूल प्रति पहले ही अपने पत्तन पर प्रस्तुत कर दी गई है अथवा उसका उपयोग कर लिया गया है तो उसकी 
सूचना तत्काल ही इस कार्यालय को भेजी जाए । 

लाईसेस फा म्यारा निम्न प्रकार है

Seamless Carbon Steel Tubes and Cold Drawn permissible sizes against exports under A. 66 - 2( A) and A. 66 - 2( B ), They have now applied 
for duplicate copy of Customspurposo copy of the abovo liconco on the ground that the original customs purposc copy of the above licenco 
hasbocn lost/misplaced after having boop registered with tho Customs House , Bombay and utilised partially . The total amount for which 
the licence was issued is Rs. 35142 and the totalamount for which theduplicate required is to cover the balance of Rs . 8312. 


In support of this contention the applicants have filed an affidavit on stamped paper duly attested before the Registrar and Metropo 
litan Magistrato, Bombay. 


I am satisfied that the original Customs purpose copy of licence No. 2728085 dated 10 - 2 -76 has been lost or misplaced and direct that 
duplicate customs purposo copy of the above licence should be issued to the applicant. The original customs purpose copy 2728085 dated 
10-2-1976 is canoelled .

ग्रामीण पुननिर्माण मंत्रालय 
नई दिल्ली , 30 नवम्बर , 1979 


का०मा० 4011. ---जीरा श्रेणीकरण और चिल्लांकन नियम , 1969 
में संशोधन करने के लिए नियमों का एक प्रारूप , कृषि उपज ( श्रेणीकरण 

और चिह्नांकन ) अधिनियम , 1937 ( 1937 का 1 ) की धारा 3 
द्वारा यया अपेक्षित रूप में , भारत सरकार के कृषि और सिंचाई मंत्रालय 
( ग्रामीण विकास विभाग ) की अधिसूचना संख्या कापा. 3319 तारीख 
30 मितम्बर, 1977 के अधीन भारत के राजपत्र , भाग 2, खण्ड 3, 
उपखण्ड ( दो ) तारीख 22 अक्तूबर, 1977 के पृष्ठ 3768 पर प्रकाशित 
किया गया था , जिसमें उक्त अधिसूचना के राजपत्र में प्रकाशन की 
बारीख से पेंतातीम दिनों की अवधि को समाप्ति तक उन सभी व्यक्तियों 
से आपत्तिा गोर सुझाव मारे गए थे , जिनके उससे प्रनावित होने की 
सम्भावना है । 

और उन राजपत्र 22 आतूबर, 1977 को जनता को उपलब्ध 
करा दिया गया था ; 

और केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उक्त प्रारूप को धाबत प्राप्त आपत्तियों 
और सुझावों पर विचार कर लिया है ; 

अत., अत्र, केन्द्रीय सरकार , उस अधिनियम की धारा 3 द्वारा 
प्रान गलितों का प्रयोग करते हुए , जोरा श्रेगोकरण और चिह्नांकन 
नियम , 1969 में संगोवन करने के लिए निम्नलिखित नियम बनाती है , 
अर्यात् : - - 


INo . CMD /13 : 12] 
A . P . BANERJI, Dy. Dir . General

का० प्रा० 4010 ---- उद्योग (विकास एवं विनियमन ) अधिनियम , 1951 
( 1951 का 65 ) की धारा 6 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते 
हुए विकास परिषद् ( कार्यविधिक ) नियमावली, 1951 के नियम 2, 
4, एवं 5 के साथ पढ़ते हुए तथा भारत सरकार के उद्योग मंत्रालय , 
प्रौद्योगिक विकास विभाग के का०पा० 5- 6/ 78-मीमेंट दिनांक 24 
सितम्बर , 1979 के आदेश के प्रांशिक संशोधन में केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्द्वारा 
श्री कमलजीत सिंह, प्रबन्धक निदेशक , एसोसियेटेड, कम्पनीज लिमिटेड , 
बम्बई के स्थान पर अध्यक्ष , सीमेंट मैन्यूफैक्चरर्स एसोसियेशन एक्सप्रेस

( मंशोधन ) निरन , 1979 है ।

निम्नलिखित कप्त संख्या प्रविष्टि और टिप्पण अन्तःस्थापित किया जाएगा ,

MINISTRY OF RURAL RECONSTRUCTION 

New Delhi, the 30th November, 1979 


S . O . 4011 .. - -Whereas the draft rules to amend the Cuminseed 
Grading and Maiking Rules, 1969, were published as required 
by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grading and 
Marking) Act, 1937 ( 1 of 1937 ), at page 3768 of the Gazette 
of India , Part II, Section 3 , Sub - section (ii), dated 22nd 
October , 1977 , with the notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Agriculture and Irrigation (Depart 
ment of Rural Development) No . S . O . 3319 , dated 30th 
September , 1977 , inviting objections and suggestions from all 
persons likely to be affected thereby within a period of fourty 
five days from the date on which the copies of the Gazette 
containing the said notification were made available to the 
public ; 

And whereas the said Gazette was made available to the 
public on the 22nd October, 1977 ; 

And whereas objections and suggestions received from the 
public on the said draft have been considered by the Central 
Government ; 

Now , therefore , in exercise of the powers conferred by 
section 3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby 
makes the following rules to amend the Cuminseed Grading 
and Marking Rules, 1969 , namely : -- 


का०मा० 4012.-~- अदरक श्रेणीकरण और विहन नियम , 1964 में 
और संशोधन करने के लिए कतिाय नियमों का निम्नलिखित प्रारूप , 
जिसे केन्द्रीय सरकार, कृषि उपज ( श्रेणीकरण और चिह्नन ) अधिनियम, 
1937 ( 1937 का 1 ) को धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग 
करते हुए , बनाने की प्रस्थापना करती है, उता धात द्वारा यथा अपेक्षित 
उन सभी व्यक्तियों की जानकारी के लिए प्रारित किया जाता है जिनका 
उसके द्वारा प्रभावित होना सम्भाव्य है और सूचना दी जाती है कि उक्त 
प्रारूप पर , उस तारीख से , जिसको उस राजपन की प्रतियां, जिसमें 
यह अधिसूचना प्रकाशित की जाती है , जनता को उपलब्ध कराई जाती 
है , तीस दिन को अवधि कि समाप्ति पर या उसके पश्चात् विचार किया 
जाएगा । 

उक्त प्रारूप की बाबत किसी भी व्यक्ति से इस प्रकार विनिर्दिष्ट 
अवधि की समाप्ति से पूर्व प्राप्त किसो भी प्रापत्ति या सुझाय पर केन्द्रीय 
सरफार विचार करेगी । 

नियमों का प्रारूप 
1. इन नियमों का नाम अदरक श्रेणीकरण और चिह्ननन ( संशोधन ) 
नियम , 1979 है । 
___ 2 अदरक श्रेणीकरण और चिहन निगम , 1964 में , अनुसूची 10 
में , स्तम्भ 5 के शोषक में , अधिकतन शब्द के स्थान पर न्यूनतम 
शब्द रखा जाएगा । 

[ सं० 13 ( 9 ) - 73 - ए० एन०] 


RULES 
1. These rules may be called the Cum vseeds Grading 

and Marking (Amendment) Rules , 1979 . 
2 . In the Cuminseeds Grading and Marking Rules, 1969. 

in Schedule II , in column 1 , after serial number 
4 and the entries relating thereto , the following 
serial number , entry and note shall be inserted . 
namely : 


S . O . 4012. - - The following draft of certain rules further to 
amend the Ginger Grading and Marking Rules , 1964 , which 
tthe Central Government purpose to make, in exercise of the 
powers conferred by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce 
(Grading and Marking) Act, 1937 ( 1 of 1937) is hreby publish 
ed , as required by the said section , for the information of all 
persons likely to be affected thereby and notice is hereby 
given that the said draft will be taken into consideration on 
or. after the expiry of a period of thriiy days from the date 
on which copies of the Official Gazette in which this noti. 
fication is published are made available to the public . 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received from 
any person with respect to the said draft before the expiry 
of the period so specified will be taken into consideration by 
the Central Government. 

DRAFT RULES 
1. These rules may be calied the Ginger Grading and 

Marking ( Amendment ) Rules , 1979 . 
2 . In the Ginger Grading and Marking Rules, 1964, in 

Schedule X , in the heading to column 5 , for the 
word " Maximum " , the word, " Minimun" , shall be 
substituted .

का० बा0 40 13. ---तम्बाकू श्रेणीकरण और चिह नांकन नियम , 1937 में संशोधन करने के लिए नियम का एक प्रारूप , कृषि उपज ( श्रेणोकरण और चिहनांकन ) 
अधिनिमय 193 ( 1937 का 1 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा यथा अपेक्षित भारत सरकार के कृषि और सिंचाई मंत्रालय ( ग्राम विकास विभाग ) की अधिसूचना संख्या 
का० मा० 3165 तारीख 21 अक्तूबर, 1978 के अधीन, भारत सरकार के राजपत्र भाग 2, खण्ड 3, उपखण्ड (ii ) तारीख 4 नवम्बर 1978 तक पृष्ठ 
3055 से 3165 ( अ ) पर प्रकाशित किया गया था , उस राजपत्र की जिसमें उक्त अधिसूचना प्रकाशित की गई थी , प्रति जनता में उपलबध हो पाने की 
तारीख से 45 दिन की अवधि की समाप्ति होने तक जिसके द्वारा उन सभी व्यक्तियों से आपत्तियां और सुझाव मांगे गए थे, जिनके उससे प्रभावित होने की 
सम्भावना है । 


और उक्त राजपब 4 नवम्बर, 1978 को जनता को उपलब्ध करा दिया गया था ; 


और केन्द्रीय सरकार ने जनता से प्राप्त आपत्तियों और सुझाव पर विचार कर लिया है ;

(ii ) अनुसूची 2 के बाप, निम्नलिखित अनुसूची मन्तःस्थापित की जाएगी, अर्थात :--- 

मनुसूची 20 
भारत में उत्पादित विनिर्मित फ्ल्यू योर्ड वर्जीनिया (पिटाई के बाद का पनवल ) सम्बाकू का श्रेणी प्रभिधान और वर्णम । 

1. पिटा हमा पत्रदल फल्यूक्योई वर्जीनिया प्रमाति की और उसकी संकर किस्मों की जिनके अभिलक्षण एक जैसे है, श्रेणीकृत तम्बाक को पीटकर प्राप्त 
किया जाता है और इसमें पत्तों के ऐसे सिरे , जो पसे की लगभग 1/ 3 लम्बाई को काटकर प्राप्त होते हैं और सिरा-कटी पत्ती ( बचा हुमा भाग ) की मंत्र 
ारा पीटकर प्राप्त भिन्न-भिन्न लम्बाइयों के प्रोकिन विट्स या टुकड़े है । 

2. पिटे गए पलपल में , पत्तों के सिरे और सिरे कटे पत्तों से प्राप्त विभिन्न लम्बाई के ऐसे प्रोकिन विट्स या दुकड़े है, जो यंत्रों में तोड़े गए है , किन्तु 
उसकी निम्नलिखित शत हैं :--- 

( क ) पते के सिरों का प्राकार लम्बाई में 3 से 6 के बीच होगा । 
( ख ) सिर कटे पत्तों से प्राप्त प्रोफिन बिट्स या टुकड़ों का प्राकार लम्बाई में 1/ 4" से 6 के बीच होगा । 
( ग ) पत्तों के सिरों का भार कुल पत्रवल के भार के 25 प्रतिशत से अधिक नहीं होगा । 
( प ) पिटे हुए पत्नवल में 1/ 2" से अधिक लम्बे टुकड़े कुल उत्पाद के कम से कम 70 प्रतिशत ( भार के अनुसार ) होंगे । 
( 1 ) पटे हुए पन्नवल में 1/ 4 * के प्रकार से कम के टुकड़े कुल उत्पाद के 10 प्रतिशत ( भार के प्रमुसार ) से अधिक नहीं होंगे । 
( ब ) पिटे हुए पत्रदल में , स्टेम का कुल भार कुल उत्पाद के 5 प्रतिशत से अधिक नहीं होगा । 
३. पिटे हुए पत्रदल का निर्यात और पैकिंग निम्नलिखित शतों पर होगी : 
( क ) पिटे गए पत्रवल के लिए नियत की जाने वाली श्रेणी, पिटाई के लिए पूर्ण पत्ते भेजने से पहले अवधारित की जाएगो । अनुसती 2 में बसाए 

गए विनिर्मित फुलू क्योर्ड वर्जीनिया सम्बाकू को लागू होने वाला श्रेणी विनिर्देश, तम्बाकू की पिटाई संयंत्र में डालने से पहले, प्रवर्तित किया जाएगा । 
( ब ) किसी विशिष्ट श्रेणी के लिए मिरीक्षित और अनुमोदित तम्बाकू की ही निरीक्षण अधिकारी की उपस्थिति में , पिटाई करने की अनुमति दी जाएगी 

पौर एगमार्क लेवल पर "पिटा हा पचवल " पर कोष्ठक में तथा श्रेणी अभिधान चिह म के साथ अंकित किया जाएगा । 
( ग ) एगमार्क बिहनाकित प्रविनिर्मित फ्ल्यू क्योर वर्जीनिया तम्बाकू के पैकेजों की भांति ही , श्रेणीकृत "पिटा हुमा पबदल " तम्बाकू के पैकेजों के भी 

प्रायिक जाँच नमूने लिए जाएंगे और जोष निरीक्षण किया जाएगा । 
( ii ) अनुसूची III के पश्चात् निम्नलिखित अनुसूची पन्तःस्थापित की जाएगी, प्रात् : ---

का० प्रा० 4014. – केन्द्रीय सरकार कृषि उपा ( श्रेणीकरण और चिकन ) अधिनियम, 1937 (1937 का 1 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों 
का प्रयोग करते हुए काली मिर्च ( गीकरण और चिन्हातान नियन, 1960 से पति और संगोपन करता याहो है । जैगा कि उक्त धारा में अपेक्षित है , 
प्रस्तावित संशोधनों का निम्नलिखित प्रारूप उन सभी प्रतिया को जानकारी के लिए TT का जा रहा है, जिनके उस पवित हाने की सम्भावना 
है । इसके द्वारा सूचना दी जाती है कि उक्त प्राम पर इस अभियान के राजा से प्रकाशन को नारीख में पंसालिम दिन के पश्चात् विचार किया जाएगा । 

कार विनिदिन सारो से पूर्व नियमों के उक्त नाम की घाबत जो भी यापेस या शुमार कितो कि से प्रान हांगे, केन्द्राय सरफार उन पर विचार 
करेगी ।

SO 4014 . The following draft of certain rules to amend the Pepper Grading and Marking Rules, 1969, which the Central Govornment 
Wanges to mako, in exercise of the powers conferred ,by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking ) Act, 1937 (1 of 1937). 
Is hereby published , as required by the said section , for the information of all persons likely to be affected thereby and notico is hereby given 
hat the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after the expiry of fortyfive days from the date on which the Official Gazetto contain 
ing this notification is made available to the public. 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received from any persons with respect to the said draft before the date 80 specified will 
be considered by the Central Government. 

DRAFT RULES : 
1. (i) These rules may be called the Pepper Grading and Marking ( Amendment ) Rules , 1979 . 

2. (i) In the Pepper Grading and Marking Rules, 1969, ( herein after referred to as the said rules ) for rule 4, the following rule shall bo 
substituted , namely : 

" 4 . Definition of quality . The quality indicated by the respective grade designation shall be as set out against each grado desime 
tion in columns (2 ) to (5 ) in Schedules II and III/columos (2 ) to (4 ) in Schsdulos IV and V / columns (2) and (3) in Schedules VI and 
VII and columns ( 2) to ( 6) in Schedules VIII and IX ".

MINISTRY OF LABOUR 

ORDER 
New Delhi, the 22nd November, 1979 
80 4018 .. .. Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that an industrial dispute exists between the employers in rela 
tion to tac management of Paradip Port Trust, at and post 
Office Paradip Port, District Cuttack, Orissa and ther work 
ment in respect of the matters specified in the Schedule hereto 
annexed , 

And whercas the Central Government considers it desirable 
to refer the said dispute for adjudication , 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sec . 
tion 7A and clause ( d ) of sub - section ( 1 ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) ,the Central Govern 
ment hereby constitutes an Industrial Tribunal of which Shri 
MV Gangaraju shall be the Presiding Officer with headquar 
ters at Bhubaneshwar and refers the said dispute for adjudica 
tion to the said Tribunal 

SCHEDULE 
Whether the action of the management of Paradip Port 

Trust, in terininating the services of Shri PC Mallick , 
Operatol , Grade III , with effect from the 20th Sep 
tember , 1976 , is justified ? If not, to what relief 18 
the concerned workman entitled ? 

[ No L- 38012 / 1 / 79- D . IV( A )]

1948 ( 1948 मा 34 ) को धारा 88 व्वारा प्रदत्त शक्लियो का प्रयोग 


श्रम मंत्रालय 

मावेश 
मई दिल्ली , 22 मवम्बर, 1979 


का०मा० 4018 - - केन्द्रीय सरकार की राय है कि इसमें उपाबद

2. पूर्णोक्त छूट की शमें निम्नलिखित हैं , पर्थात् : -- 

( 1 ) उक्त कारखाने का नियोजक , उस अवधि की पाबत जिसके 
दौरान उस कारखाने पर उपत पधि नियम प्रवर्तमान था (जिसे इसमें इमले 
प्रश्चात् उक्त प्रयधि कहा गया है ), ऐसी विवरणियां , ऐसे प्रकप में और 
ऐसी विशिष्टियों सहित देगा जो फर्मचारी राज्य बीमा ( साधारण ) 
विनियम , 1950 के अधीन उसे उक्त प्रधि की बाबत देनी थी ; 

( 2 ) निगम द्वारा उक्त पधिनियम की धारा 45 की उपधारा (1 ) 
के अधीन नियुक्त किया गया कोई निरीक्षक , या निगम का इस निमित्त 
प्राधिकृत कोई अन्य पक्षधारी --- 
( 1 ) धारा 44 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के पधोन , उक्त अवधि को बापत

प्रयोजनार्थ; या i

हैं ; या

कर्मचारी है, परीक्षा करना ; या

"Whether the employeos of Dalmia Magnesito Corpora 

tion , Salem are ontitled to payment of rest day and 

if so what should be their Sunday wages" . 
2 . Three Uniong ospouse the cause of the workmen and 
had Aled three separate claim statement in February , March , 
and April , 1971. The Management of M : . Daimia Magno 
site Corporation Salem filed their counter statement on 
24 -4 - 1971 , The workmen and management placed oral and 
documentary evidence on all the materials. My learned pre 
decessor Thiru K . Seetharama Rao rendered an award on 
30 - 1 - 1971, to the effect that the attendance bonus schome 
has now prevalent in Burn and Company Limited will be 
given to the worker in this company from the date when this 
award is published in the Gazette and that the payment to be 
made is as per the attendance bonus scheme owards the rest 
day wages. The Respondent Management challenged the 
Award of this Tribunal by filing writ petition No . 476 of 1972 
on the flo of the High Court, Madras on 6 - 1 - 76 . The High 
Court dismissed the said writ petition confirming the award 
passed by this Tribunal. Not satisfied with thig Order of the 
High Court, the Management filed writ appeal No. 80 of 
1976 on the file of the High Court, Madras. That appeal was 
decessor Thiru K . Seetharama Rao rendered an award on 
6 - 7 - 1978 . Eventually the Division Bench allowed the appeal 
And set aside the order passed in writ potition No. 476 of 
1972 and quashed the award of this Tribunal dated 30 - 10- 1971 
and directed this Tribunal to reconsider the entire matter 
afresh with reference to the actual disputo referred to it . 


3 . The only point that has to be considered in whether 
the employees of Dalmia Magnesito Corporation , Salem aro 
entitled to the payment for the rest day and if so what should 
be their Sunday wages. It is common ground that the Res 
pondent Managoment is carrying on the business of mining 
of Magnesite and manufacture of Dead Burn Magnesite em 
ploying about 800 workers directly who are covered by the 
present reference besides somo independent contractors and 
other staff . The workmen worked for six days in a week 
and Sunday is a day off weekly rest for thcm , Although the 
three unions representing the workmon employed by the Reg 
pondent Company had filed separate claim statements, all of 
them are united in this namely requesting that an award may 
be passed for mining wages for rest day namely Sunday at 
a rate of usual current normal rates for a working day. The 
Defence put forward by the management can be gathered 
from paragraph 8 of the claim statement Aled by the Rea 
pondent Management. 


4 . Thoir contentions are two - fold , In the fist place the 
daily rate of wages already givon to the workorn duly take 
care of the wages for the weekly day off rest. Secondly , as 
the provision of the minimum Wages Act have beçn extended 
to the Magnesite industry in case the existing rates of wages , 
were found to fall short of the minimum rates of the wages , 
the game will be raised and that therefore , there was no 
justification to press the demand for payment of wages for 
the weekly day off rest ,

worked out by dividing the minimum monthly rato

( In 1961 II LLJ the Supreme Court had stated as follows : 
" We express no opinion as to what the position of law is 
after this amendment of rule 23 ) . 


5 . Ex . C - 1 is a notification dated 1st July , 1970 of the 
Government of India adding the employment of Magnesite 
Mines to Part 1 of the Schedule to tbe minimum wages Act 
( Centre ) . Therefore even as per this notification the Minimum 
Wages Act had been extended to the employment in the 
Magnesite Mincs also with cffect from 1 - 7 - 1970 . That apart 
exibit M -4 is the copy of the notification of Central Govern 
ment regarding fixation of minimum rate of wages to the 
employment in Magnesite Mines. The explanation attached 
to this notification states that the minimum rates proposed are 
all inclusive rates , including basic wages , the cost of living 
wlowances, the cash value of the concessions supplied if any 
value of essential commodities and also include wages payable 
for the weekly rest day. Therefore , it is sought to be con 
tended that when minimum wages are to be paid under tho 
Minimum Wages Act, 1948, then as per the explanation the 
inclusive of wages payable for the weekly rest day and there 
fore the workmen will not be entitled to anything additionally 
by way of wages for weekly rest day. But, it is significant 
to note that even in the counter statement filed by the Manage 
ment on 24 - 4 - 1971 it is not stated that the workmen are 
paid wages in accordance with Minimum Wages Act of 1949 . 
The Management has also not filed any additional counter 
after demand by High Court to say that the wages paid to 
the workmen are in accordance with or above the wages 
fixed under the Minimum Wages Act, 1948. 


6 . I shall now examine the first contention of the Manage 
ment namely that the daily rate of wages duly take care of 
the wages for the weekly day off the rest. Exibit M - 1 is the 
award passed by this Tribunal in I. D . 39 of 1966 dated 
27-6 - 1968 . The dispute in those I, Ds covered the workmen 
of the Respondent Management also . Eventually in I. D . 39 
of 1966 this Tribunal fixed the rate of wages for the workers 
of Respondent Management. The Tribunal fixed the monthly 
wages of tho worker on the following lines : 


" To arrive at monthly wages , the above Jaily wages 

shall be multiplied by 26 the number representing 
the number of working days in a month , Similarly , 
the increment per month should be calculated and 
the two sums totalled to arrive at total monthly 
wagos of the different categories of workmen " . He 
then fixed the dearness allowance payable for each 
category of employees" . 


10 . In the first place as I have earlier pointed out it is 
not even the stand of the management that the workers are 
paid minimum wages or above the minimum wages under 
thç Minimum Wages Act. Secondly , in the earlier Award 
fixing the wages in 1. D . 39 of 1966 the monthly rate of wages 
had not been first determined and thereafter dividing the same 
by 26 to fix daily wages . Hence , no reliance whatsoever can 
be placed on Rule 23 of the Minimum Wages Central Rule 
1950 . Industrial Law in our country has clearly come to a 
stage to take the view that where two judicial choices are 
plausible the construction in conformity with the social, 
philosophy of Article 44 of our Constitution has to be pre 
ferred . In thc light of these circumstances I am unable to 
accept the contention of the Management that the workmen 
of the Management will not be entitled to payment of wages 
for weekly day off rest. It should also be remembered that 
the Management does not dispute that every workmen is en 
titled to a day off rest after six days of work . The financial 
burden on the Management consequent on the demands under 
the reference has also to be considered . The Salem district 
Magnesite Labour Union in paragraph 9 of the claims state 
ment had mentioned that the Management have the capacity 
to meet this demand and the monthly commitment in this 
regard will not be more than Rs. 10 , 000 . In paragraph 9 of 
the Counter statement, the Respondent Company " denies the 
allegations that the financial burden on the management, as 
a result of the demand under reference , is only small and 
the management capable of meeting the same" . But significan 
tly the Management has not challenged the claim of the union 
that the additional commitment would ongy be in the region 
of Rs. 10 , 000 per month . No doubt the Respondent Manage 
ment had stated that in the event of payment of wages for 
the rest separately the liability of the Respondent Company 
will increase not only the payment of the wage bill but also 
in other respects as well as Provident Fund , Bonus, Leave 
pay etc . But oven thon the Respondent Management did not 
contend that it can ill afford to bear this additional commit 
ment if ordered . It must be mentioned that tho learned Counsel 
for the Respondent Management did not address any argument 
on the financial burden that may be cast on the Respondent 
management in the event of the payment of wages for the 
rest day being allowed . The Respondent management which 
is a reputed Company of long record has capacitated to bear 
this additional burden. Magnesite in Salem district is being 
mined by three companies namely the Respondent manage 
ment Dalmia magnesite Corporation and Bum and Company 
Limited and Salem Magnesite Private Limited . It transpires 
in evidence that each of these three companies is earning same 
percentage of profits on about the game investment. Actually 
the Respondent Corporation is said to be bigger in the matter 
of investments and profits than Salem Magnesite Private Limi 
ted . The attendanco bonus scheme was in vogue in Burn and 
Company . Although Attendance bonus schemo may not be 
strictly equated to a weekly rcst day wage , in effect the afore 
said company was also making payment of wages for the 
reat day . Therefore , it is apparent that in the samc region 
two of the magnesito companies with no better profits than 
the Respondent management are literally paying rest day wages 
to their employees . Those facts can also be taken into congi 
deration on the basis of the region wise industry . That would 
also justify the ordering of the payment of the workers of 
the Respondent management off weekly rest day . Looked at 
from many point of view , a case is made out for payment 
of wages for rest days to the workers of the Respondent 
Management. This will be at the rate of their usual daily 
wage inclusive of Dearness Allowance and other allowances, 


7 . On behalf of the management, the learned counsel Thiru 
Govindaswaminathan pointed out that in the aforesaid award 
the monthly wages bad been determined by multiplying daily 
wages by 26 and therefore the wages pald for a month must 
be held to include rest days also and as such the workmen 
will not be entitled to any separate wages for rest days as 
claimed by them . It must be remembered that in the award 
I. D . 39 of 1966 the total wages earned by the worker for 
cach month had been arrived at. In 1961 II L . L .J . page 632 
(Between Workmen of Bombay Port Trust and Trustees of 
the Port of Bombay ) the Supreme Court considered the issue 
about wages for the weekly rest day. In that case also the 
Management raisod an identical contention like this that the 
workmen were paid monthly wages, wages constructively for 
the offs also . This view has been repelled by the Supreme 
Court at page 637 and this is how they had ended this finding 
" By no stretch of imagination can payment for six days be 
cquated for payment for seven days " . 


8 . On behalf of the workmen reliance is placed on the 
Jutest decision of the Supreme Court reported in 1978 
I L . L .J . page 29 (Betwcen B . Shah and Labour Court, Colm 
batore and others) where the question was raised as to how 
to compute maternity benefit for a female worker engaged on 
a daily wage basis : Whether a wageless Sunday should be 
included or should it be excluded ? Their lordships held a 
" weck " has to he taken to signify a cpcle of seven days 
including Sunday . The benefit conferred must be extended 
to Sundays also . Although that decision might not directly 
be applied to the facts of the present case , it will throw 
considerable light as how i week should be considered in 
cluding a Sunday a rost day. 


9 . Learned Counsel for management relies on the amended 
Rule 23 of Minimum Wages Central Rule 1950 which reads 
as follows : 


12. That leads me to the consideration to the final question

counter statement, the management has stated in any event 
under no circumstances the award can be given effect from 
1970 or any other carlier date as claimed . The date of referonco

3 . The several circumstances preceded the aforesaid setile 
ment under Ex. M - 14 had been set out in paragraph 9 , 10 
11 , 12 and 13 of the counter statement filed by the Respon 
dent- -Management. Most of the facts are not contradicted 
as per the settlement Ex. M - 14 . The workers should exca 
vate the load of 5 metric tonnes per head per shift and the 
workers will receive an additional amount at the rate of Rs. 
4 )- per mctric tonnes limit and there will be proportionate 
doduction for short load below that figure. Payment deduc 
tion of this amount shall be calculated once in every calender 
month and will be based on collective output per shift. There 
fore it is clear from the aforesaid terms that the workmen 
have to necessarily turn out five metric tonnen load per head 
per shift and if per chance they had not reached that figure 
they will be pald only proportinoatcly , 


4 . After conclusion of the settlement for the first three 
months the workmen were doing their work in the agreed 
fashion but subsequently they began to lag behind their 
agreed target. The workmen were not turning out the work 
As per the agreement in tho months of May , June, July and 
August, 1976 . However , at the intervention of the Collector 
of Triunelveli District tho Respondent-Management had pald 
tho full wages in the month of September and October , 1976 , 
although the workmen did not give the normal out turn , Fur 
ther more the work turned out by the workers for the months 
of November and December , 1976 and January , 1977 were bo 
low five metrlo tonnas por head per day and they were pald by 
tho Management only according to the work turned out by 
cach worker. Therefore , from 1 - 12 - 1976 upto 31- 12 - 1976 
the Managoment had doducted tho wages of workmen on the 
ground of short fall likewise the Management deducted the 
wagcs of workmen from 1- 2 - 1977 upto 31- 3 - 1977. Thus the 
abort point for appreciation would be whether there are 
short fall in tho out turn of work in the light of the settle 
ment Ex . M - 14 , entored into on 25- 1 - 1976 ? It appears in evi 
dendo that the Management has deducted the wages for 
short fall only from November, 1976 . According to the 
Management thery was short fall even in the months of May , 
Juno, July , August, 1976 and the Management had agreed 
to pay the full wages for September and October , 1976 as a 
gesturo of good will on the intervention of the District Col 
loctor, Tirunelveli. However the Management had paid the 
wages to the workmon strictly in accordance with the terms 
of the settlement with effect from Novembor, 1976 . The ques . 
tion is whether the action of the Management is justificd. 
Altough the dispute ts sponsored by the thrce Unions, Union 
No . 3 did not filc any claim statement and other two filed 
a claim statement (Union No . 1 and 2 ) . Union No. 1 and 
3 did not ovinco any interest in the present dispute. Learned 
counsel for Union No . 2 alono participated in the enquiry 
with regard to this issue . He has frankly admitted that tho 
workinen would stand only by the terms of settlement arriv 
ed at under EL M - 14 , dated 20 - 1 -1976 and conceded that tho 
Management is entitled to pay wagos to the workmep 
strictly in accordance with the terms of the settlement Ex . 
M - 14 , But his only submission is the short load put forward 
by the Management is not correct. No where in the claims 
statement filed either by his union or Union No. 1, Is there 
any whisper that the workmen had in fact turned out tho 
work lo accordance with the terms of settlement Ex. M - 14 . 
Their only grievance is that the weighment of the load made 
by the Respondent Management is not correct. Significan 
tly they do not gay or contend that they have increased the 
out turn as agreed to under the terms of the Settlement Ex. 
M - 14 . Adtnittedly he Respondent Management had no 
weighbridge of his own and therefore the workmon had 
agreed before the District Collector, Tirunelveli that the 
lorry ghould be weigbed in India Cements Limited . It is 
not the case of workmen that the loaded lorries were not 
weighed in Indfa Cements weighbridge. Therefore it had to 
We seen whether the load as calculated by the Management 
to correct and can be accepted. Ex. M - 19 serles are log 
shoots from 1- 11- 1976 upto 31- 12 - 1976 . Ex . M - 20 series 
are weighment advico from 1 - 11- 1976 to 31- 12 - 1976 . Et. 
M -21 serice contain daily performance figures from 1 -6 - 1976 


5 . Learned counsel for the Management has clearly point 
out that he has produced these documents only as samples to 
show how the load is weighed at Cement Weighbridge. Little 
purpose will be served by producing thro entire documents. 
But from the above unimpeachable materials it can be seen , 
that the weighment of Lime stones supplied by the workmen 
had tho approval of the authority of the Revenue Department 
as well as the Excise Department of tho Central Governmont 
who were respectively collecting royalty and exciso duty . All 
these registers would also indicate that these registers had 
becn checked by the officials of the Revenue and Excise De 
partment. It is true that at some stage the workmen refused 
to subscribe his signature to the weighment made of tho 
India Cements weighbridge . However , the Respondent 
Management had no other option but to continue to do the 
weighment even in the absence of the workmen . Tho chiet 
complalot of the Union is that weighment was not donc in 
the presence of the workmen and thereforo irrespective of 
thre out turn given by them , the workmen had to be paid in 
full. I am afraid this submission cangot be accepted , Learned 
counsel for the Union would also point out that the weigh 
ment in the absence of the work would be violation of rulok 
relating to Payment of Wages Mines Rules , 1956 . If any 
proceedings are launched against the Respondent-Management 
for failure to observe the rules they will have to face tho 
consequences but the hard fact remaing that the actual load 
turned out by the workmon had been recorded at India 
Cements Weighbridge and accepted by the Management. It 
is not denied that weighment slips obtained from the India 
Cements was pastod in the Notico Board of the Respondent 
Management every time. Significantly at no time was any 
objection taken to correctness of the weighment noted in 
those slips . This claim of the Management in paragraph 16 
of their counter statement does not ovoke any reply or re 
joinder statement from any of the 3 Unions refuting it. Tho 
actual weight had been vouch gafed both by the Revenue and 
Central Exciso Department . In the face of those overwhelma 
ing materials, there íg absolutely no ground to reject the case 
of the Management that with effect from November, 1976 
also , the workmen did not turn out in accordance with the 
terms of settlement under Ex, M - 14 and the workmen woro 
accordingly paid for the actual work turned out by them . 
In that view the action of the Management in having paid 
wages to the workmen from November, 1976 strictly in 
accordance with the termg Ex, M - 14 cannot be held to bo 
unjustified. The workmen are not entitled to any relief, 


6 . In the result, an Award is passed dismissing tho claim 
with regard to Issue ( 1 )(b ). No cont. 
Dated , this sth day of November, 1979. 
T, SUDARSANAM DANIAL, Presiding Officer,

4 . Rcgarunig paid festival boliduys I do not think any

in fact impart from the affidavit no other evidence has been

or Industrin establishments of the area except hose statuarily 
vovered , The workmen have also failed to show figures 
of their output and production or to bring out the financial 
burden or expenditure which would be involved Y a prac 
tice to grant festival holidays is introduced . While agreeing 
that there is ncod for standardisation of service conditions 
including those relating to grant of festival holidays. I am 
afraid time is not yet ripe to grant festival holidays except 
where there are agreements between the parties, Tbo work 
men have not cotablished that there are any standing orders 
granting any festival holidays . The model standing orders 
under The Industrial Employment ( Standing Orders ) Act, 
1946 apparently are not applicable to these workmen , Keep 
ing in vicw of these facts I am of the opinion that no 
litse has been made out for grant of my paid festival holi 
days in thosc Sand Stone Mines and accordingly I hold 
that the workmen are not entitled to any paid holidays over 
and above these three national holidays awarded above .

3 . From the perugal of the various statemcots of claim 
fi cd against the Managements by the workmen I find that 
the contention of the workmen is that they had raised tho 
demands for grant of 10 festival holidays to these workmen 
which demand was not even acknowledged much less granted .

oncc has been mado . The same thing is stated on affidavit 
by Shri Mahabir Parshad Sharma and further Shri Mahabir 
Parshad Sharma has claimed that 10 festival holidays should 
be granted to the workmen working in these various mincs .

of things that tho workmen are awarded thrce paid national 
holidays namely 26th January, on account of Republic day, 
15th August on account of Independence day and 2nd Octo 
ber on account of Mahatma Gandhi Jayanti, The employers 
would be liable to grant these three national holidays to 
the workmen and it is awarded that the workmen of theso 
Sand Stone Mines arc entitled to grant these thrco national 
holidays above mentioned i.e . 26th January , 13th August and 
2nd October in a year . I am not oblivious of the fact that 
the workmen have not produced any evidence to suggest that 
these holidays are being allowed in any other concern in tho 
neighbourhood . However I am prompted to grant the bene 
fit to these three national holidays by the fact that this would 
enable these workers in Sand Stone Mines to join the main 
stream of the nation in celebrating these days of national 
importance. It would have the effect of a national Integration 
25 well .

case has been made out by the workmen for grant thereof. 
In fact apart from the affidavit no other evidence has been 
led to show by the workmen that there is any agreement 
betwcen the workmen and the Management in this behall . 
They have also not led any evidence to establish that there 
is any practice of granting festival holidays in other mines 
or Industrial establishments of the arca exccpt those statutarl 
ly covered . The workmen have also failed to show figures 
of their output and production or to bring out the financial 
burden or expenditure which would be involved if a practice 
to grant festival holidays is introduced . While agrccing that 
there is necd for standardisation of service conditions inclu 
ding those relating to grant of festival holidays , I am afraid 
time is not yet ripe to grant festival holiday , except there 
aic agreements between the parties. The workmen have not 
established that there are any standing orders under thc In 
dustrial Employment (Standing Orders ) Act, 1946 apparently 
are not applicable to these workmen , Keeping in view of 
these facts I am of the opinion that no case has been made 
for grant of any paid festival holidays in these Sand Stono 
Mines and accordingly I hold that the workmen are not en 
titled to any paid holidays over and above these three national 
holidays awarded above .

for the charge levelled against him Ex M - 20 dated 26 - 5 - 76

enquiry on 14th , 20th and 30th July , 1976 . Five witnesses were 
cxamined on behalf of the management and these were cross 
examined by the petitioner and petitioner also examined fivo 
witnesses on his behalf. Ex. M -21 dated 9 - 8 -76 is the written

Ex. M - 22 (Ex . M -23 is only a copy of Ex, M - 22 handed over 
to the Respondent management) is the written argument filed 
on behalt of the charge sheeted workman , Ex, M - 24 are the 
findings of the enquiry officer dated 28 - 8 -76 . The enguiry 
officer on the basis of the evidence rocorded hy him and on 
tho document produced before him gavo a finding that of the 
fivo chargca levelled against the petitioner , the first throe

potitioner from the Bank Service and therefore , issi

3 . In the first place it has to be considered whether the 
domestic enquiry held is falr and proper. Even a perusal of 
the claim statement filed by the petitioner does not suggest 
that the domestic enquiry held was neither fair nor proper, 
Even under Ex. M - 28 and Fx. M -31, the petitioner has in 
detail challenged the domestic enquiry on several grounds 
but there is no whisper that he was not afforded any reason 
able opportunity to participate in the domestic enquiry conduct 
ed by the management. Five charges were levelled against the 
octitioner and they were clearly mentioned in the charge memn 
issued to the netitioner. Petitioner was given ample onportu 
nity to offer his explanation . Merely because the manngement 
did not grcept his explanation . the fact cannot be denied that 
a rcasonable opportunity had heen granted to the netitioner to 
meet his charger and he had in fert denied all of them . The 
management had exomined there witnesses and they tvere cr094 

Tamined by the nctitioner . The petitioner hat also examined 
five witncqers on his hehalf and both the mananement and the 
notitioner hart submitted written arguments viste Fr 11- 21 
Ann Ex M -22 The enquiry was held on 14th . 20th and 
30th of Tuly 1976 after the isque of notice of engulrø Fy 
M - ºn datert 26 - 5 - 76 In fact leamed counsel for the workmin 
of Thiru R . Levahmana Ayyat padrowed no argument on the 
fairnres of he domestic erguiry held and did not contend that 
principles of natural justice had not been followed in the 


conduct of the enquiry. Undor these circumstances, it is not 
difficult for me to find that the comestic cliquiry held was 
fair and proper 

4 . This leud , me to the considciation whether the domestic 
enquity suffers from any infirmaties . It is slated that enquiry 
officer himself was prejudiced against the workman and there 
fore , the enquiry held must be biased . The enquiry officer way 
one Mr. Petoj Senior A Grade Officer of the Respondent 
Banh . Merely because the enquiry officer during the cou : 30 
of enquiry regulated the proceedings it may not be concluded 
that the enquiry was biased . Assuming that tlıç enquiry officer 
had admitted certain inadmissible evidence and rejected certain 
admissible evidence, it docs not necessarily show hia , on his 
side It should also ho remembered that the petitioner had 
filcc a regular appeal against the findings of the enquiry officer 
to the appellate authority vido Ex. M -31. That appeal was 
dismissed by the appellate authority on 5 - 7 -77 ( vide Ex. M - 32 ) 
It is significant to point out that even in the lengthy ground 
of appeal Ex. M -31, there is no whisper that the coquiry 
officer was prejudiced against the petitioner to any extent. 
The domestic enquiry was held in terms of Chapter 19 of 
2nd Bl-partito settlemont Ex. M - 36 which lays down the 
procedure for taking the action . In the light of Ex. M - 36 , 11 
does not point out that the domestic enquiry held suffers from 
any infirmitics as such , At one stágo before the domestic 
enquiry officer it was stoutly urged by the workman that the 
prosecuting authority is the villain of the piccc and therefore , in 
any event he must he permitted to oxarning as the witness on 
behalf of tho workman but that request was rejected by the 
enguiry officer . However, gubsequently after the evidence of 
the prosecution and tho workman s evidence were rocorded the 
domestic enquiry officer had revised his carlier decision to put 
the prosecuting authority to be cross examined by the workman 
and the workman had cross examined the prosecuting authority 
also . Therefore , after permitting the prosecuting authority to 
bo crons examined by tho workman it cannot bo said that any 
part of the proceeding before tho enquiry officor ia vitiated 
or opposed to the principles of natural justice . In the circums 
tances, the donostic enquiry held does not suffor from any 
infirmities as such . 

5 . That leads mo to be next consideration as to whether 
that the findings of the enquiry officer are perverso or based

quiry officer has been swayed hy inadmissible materials and 
failed to consider the broad a9pects of the charges og pointed 
out carlier . Five charges were levelled against the petitioner 
Ag can be gathered from the memo trgued to the petitioner 
by the management on 18 - 11- 76 Ex. M -14. Ex. M - 24 is the 
findings of the enquiry officer. The clear finding of tho 
officer is to the effect that chargch four and five had not been 
Rubstantiated against the petitioner . It may also be remembered 
that while accepting the findings of the enquiry officer , tho 
management did not have any reservation about the two 
charges held by the enquiry officor as having not been proved . 
Thereforo , It is unnecessary for me to refer to tbe charges 
fous and five . However, it has to be seen whether with regard 
to the remaining first threo charges , any acceptablo evidence 
had been placed by tho management to substantiate thoso 
charges. . 


6 The Cates relating to those three charges are quite 
relevant. First charge is that the petitioner has misappro 
priated Rs 100 on 6 - 11- 71 . The second charge is that the 
petitioner has appropriated for himself Rs. 150 on 22- 4 - 1974

Y B . Zala and another M .P , Thakkar , J) FJR in ( 1979 ) 55

the decision which is only in accordance with the common

9 . With regard to the last charge said to be proved it

" A delay of about 11 years must be considered fatal 

from the point of view of affording reasonable 
opportunity to the comployee concerned to show cause 
against the charge levelled against him . It would 
bo asking for the impossible to expect the employee 
concerned to explain satisfactorily thc icason which 
occasioned the delay in reporting for duty . If the 
chargo or accusation had been levelled very soon 
after the lapse , the employee concerned could have 
rendered an appropriate cxplanation regardless of 
whether it was or was not cons.dered satisfactory 
by the competent authority. Not having done so 
for more than 14 years after the occuirence , the 
employce cannot be penalised for not being able to 
show cause to the satisfaction of the diciplinary 
Authority . Under the circumstances, the very delny 
in initiating proceedings must be held to constitute 
A denial of reasonable opportunity to defend him 
self for one cannot reasonably expect an employee 
to have a computer -like memory or to maintain a 
day - to -day diary in which very small matter is 
meticulously recorded in anticipation of future 
eventualities of which he cannot have a prevision 
Nor can he be expocted to adduco evidence to establish 
his innocence for after inordinate delay he would 
not recall the identity of the witnesses who could 
support him . Delay by itself, therefore , would 
constitute denial of regnon ble opportunity to show 
cause. This would amount to violation of principles 
of natural justice and the impugned order of 

removal from service must be struck down " .

2 . From the perusal of varloug orders of references it would

of the workman Shri Thakur Durga Dass ?

AWARD 
This is an Industrial Dispute between the workmen and the 
Menagement of Chettinad Cement Corporation Limited , Puli 
yur Cement Factory Post, Karur Taluk , Tiruchirapalli District 
reforred to this Tribunal for adjudication under scction 1012 ) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 by the Goscinment of Inc , 
in Order No . L -29011 / 132 /75- D . III( B ), dated 27th February , 
1976 of the Ministry of Labour , in respect of the following 
issues : 


1. Whether the fitment of different categories of workmen 

employed in Alambadi Limestone Mines of the Chet 
tined Cement Corporation Limited , Puliyus Cement 
Factory Post, Karur Taluk , Tiruchirapalli District, in 
different gradcs is proper, and if not, what modifica 
tions are necessary ? 


furnish job descriptions in respect of oach category of work

collection . It may also be noted that the Management was 
kind enough to spare the Union representatives for these pur 
poses on duty . Data was accordingly collected in Annexure - I of 
EA. M - 1 . I hereafter direct negotiations were made from 
cach workman in the presence of the Trade Union represen 
tatives and all the job doscriptions were written by the Asses

of work and skill required to perform the job , job specifica 
tions were also prepared by the Assessors. ( vido Annexure 
3 ). A job evaluation plan was eventually submitted by the 
Assessors as is contained in Annexure 4 of Ex . M - 1 . Even 
after the Assessor filed the Report Ex. M - 1 this Tribunal cal 
led upon both parties to file their objections if any to the 
job evaluation report under Ex . M - 1 submitted by the 
Madras Productivity Council. The Managcent did not filo 
any objcction to the 1950ssor s Report, whereas all tho four 
Unions filed their objections to tho Assessor s Report Ex ,

2 . Whether the demand of the worknięn employed in Alum 

badi Limestone Mines of the Chettinad Cement Cor 
poration Limited , Puliyur Cement Factory Post, Karui 
Taluk , Tiruchirapalli District, for washing allowance 
and night-shift allowance is justified ? If so , to what 
relief are the said workmen entitled ? 


( 2 ) The facts leading up to the present dispute are not in 
controversy . The Respondent is the Mangement of Chettined 
Cement Corporation Limited , Puliyur Cement Factory Pust, 
Karur Tuluk , Tiruchirapalli District. The cement factory of 
the Respondent Corporation is located at Puliyur, Karur Taluk , 
Trichy District, whereas lime-stono mines are situuled 20 miles 
away from the factory at Alambali, Vadachandur Taluk , 
Madurai District. There are about 600 workers employed by 
the Management in tho cement factory and about 150) workers 
in the lime stone mines. The present reference relates only to 
the workmen employed in Alambadi limestone mines by the 
Respondent-Management. The workmen employed by the Res 
pondent-Managenient in Alambadi limo- tone mines gie IC 
presented by 4 Unions , viz ., Chattinad Cement Corporation 
Uzhaippalar Sangam , Chettinad Cement Quarry Workers 
Union , Chettinad Cement Corporation Anna Thozhilalar Union 
and Cement Workers Progressive Union . The four Unions 
have filed separato claim statements but they are agreed in 
thrig ( ), for the fitment of the various workers in proper 
categories and (ii) for claiming night shift allowance 
and washing allowance . Since the claims of all workmen re 
presented by the four Unions are substantially identical the 
Respondent-Management has filed a common counter state 
ment contending that tho existing fitment of workmen mado 
by the Respondent -Corporation is justified and needs no 
rovision and that the workmen employed by the Respondent 
Management would not be entitled to the night shift and 
washing allowance . The Managoment thus contonded that 
tho claim with reference to issue No. 2 must be rejected 
while so far as issue No . 1 is concerned a qualified technical 
Assessor may be appointed to submit a report in respect of 
the various categories of workers and that he may also be 
directed to suggest the gradation of those categoricu. Sub 
sequently , the Management field Miscellaneous Application 
No. 19 , of 1977 under section 11 clauso 5 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 to appoint a third party independent 
Angossor with technical qualification to visit the quarries and 
gubmit A detailed report regarding the categorisation and 
gradation of various types of workmen employed in the 
quarries . This application was resisted by the Unions con 
tending that the appointment of an Assessor is not necessary 
and this is only an attempt to prolong the issue. On 17 - 2 - 1977, 
my learned predecessor passed an order appointing an Asses 
NOT to go into the question and submit the report. From 
the order, it is seen that the Management has undertaken to 
bear the cost of the Assessor and the Tribunal hag al passed 
an order that the remineration of tho Assessor will be fixed 
later and it will be paid by the Management. Consequently 
my learned predecessor appointed the Madras Productivity 
Council, Madras as An Assessor to make a study of the 
working in the quarrics and submit report relating to cate 
gorisation and gradation of various workmen with liherty 
for both parties to submit memo of instructions to Aspessor, 
The Madras Productivity Council undertook the job evalu 
ation as directed by this Tribunal of Alambadi Mine of 
Chettinad Cement Corporation Limited , Puliyor. Ex, M - 1 ls 
the Job Evaluation Report submitted by the Madras Producti 
vity Council, Even from this Report Ex. M - 1 it can be 
gatherod that firstly it was decided that Management should 


( 3 ) At this stage another significant development took place . 
While the inattet was pending before this Tribunal tho work

No. L - 29013 / 2 / 77- D - III - 8 , dated 9th January , 1978 . The ar 
hitration board rendered its Award on 26th September 1978 . 
Ex. M -6 is the printed copy of that Award . Annexure VI of 
Ex , M - 6 dealt with occupational nomenclature with job des 
criptions and their classification into grades of various work 
men employed in cement factories and quarries on a national 
basis. The question of payment of night shift allowance and 
the washing allowance had also been decided by the said 
Award of Arbitration Board — vide paragraphs 172 to 182 
and 184 The Award came into force with cffcct from 1 - 10 
1978 , That being so on 9 - 12 - 1978 , the Respondent-Manage

ment had agreed before this Tribunal to abide by the terms 
of award Ex . M -6 and to work out tho consequent details 
la discussions between the Management and the Unions and 
such discussions went ori for some time. Both the parties 
held series of bilateral disclxgions on the question of imple

limestone mines. Ex. M - 5 are the terms of settlement under 
Section 18 ( 1 ) of the Industrial Disputes Act entered into 
between the Management and the workmen represented by two 
Unions, viz ., Chettinad Cement Quanty Workers Union and 
Chettinad Cement Corporation Anna Thozhilalar Union out 
of four Unions on 13- 10 - 1979. Two Unions which did not 
subscribe to the settlement under Ex, M - 5 had filed their ob 
jections before this Tribunal. I shall now advert to the ob 
jections raised by them 


( 4 ) lo the first place these two Unions, viz ., Chettinad 
Cement Corporation Uzhaippalar Sangam (Union No. 1) and 
Cement Workers Progressive Union (Union No . 4 ) contend 
that in as much as they did not agree to the Settlement under 
Ex, M - 5, the terms of those settlements would not bind their 
workmen , Needless to point out that in ag much in the Settlo 
ment under Ex. M - S , is only one under Section 18 ( 1 ) it will 
necessarily in law bind only the parties thereto . Both these 
Unions faintly contended that in A9 much as Union No. 4 
alone had originally raised the demands roşulting in the present 
reference any settlement without its consent must be held to 
br bad in law . But this position has been repolled by a Divi 
sion Bench of our High Court in Writ Petition No. 2742 / 76 
dated 1 - 2 - 1979 reported in a matter arises from this Tribunal 
in I. D . No . 41 / 1975. Thereforo after this decision of the Divi. 
sion Bench such a contention cannot be countenanced, Tho 
claim of Union No. 4 is that the Unions which are parties 
to the Settlement Ex . M - S have very few members and that 
majority of the workmen oppose this Settlement, and their

under Ex. M - 5. Therfere even on a consideration of the 
totality of the materials now placed there is room to say that 
the majority of workmen support the settlement entered into 
under Ex. M - 5 . Although if majority of workmen support 
a settlement that can be taken into considoration as one 
factor to support the fairness and justness of the terms of 
the settlement the converse case , viz ., that merely because a 
minority of the workmen support the terms of the settlement 
it does not necessarily follow that the termsof settlement are 
not fair , unjust or unequitable . In this context one significant 
circumstance should also be bome in mind . Union No. 4 
i. e . Cement Workers Progressive Union which claims to be 
the majority Union claiming a work force of 197 19 evident 
from Ex. W - 12 had participated in the discussions held with 
the Management for 15 bearings. As a result of detailed 
discussions on 18 - 9 - 1979 three Unions including Chettinad 
Cement Corporation Quarry Workers Progressive Union had 
approved the draft terms of settlement as will be borne out 
by Ex . M -7 . It is not as though the Union representative had 
simply affixed his signature in Ex . M - 7 without agrecing to 
the terms of the settlement. Further more, even in the objec 
tion filed by the fourth Union there is no whisper that the 
Union had consented to the terms of settlement under any 
misapprehension . In the absence of any such claim , cven in 
thc objection it is perfecily clear that even the majority 
Union represented by the fourth Union had in fact agreed 
to the terms of the settlement as mentioned in Ex. M - 7 . 
The terms mentioned in Ex. M -7 are translated into details 
in the settlement Ex . M - 5 . Therefore three Unions includ 
ing fourth Union had infact consented to the terms of settle 
ment entered into under Ex . M - 7 and Ex. M - 5 . In that 
view Union No. 4 is also bound by the terms of settlement 
catered into under Ex. M -7 and M - 5 . In as much as on the 
facts appearing in this dispute substantial majority of the 
workmen have supported the torms of settlement under Exs. 
M - 7 and M - S it is not unreasonable to hold that the terms of 
settlement entered into under Ex. M - 5 are just , fair and 
equtable . 


of the Award of Arbitration Board for Comont Industry as 
scop from clause 207 of Ex, M -6 must bave to be implementod , 
In that ylew when the greater majority of the workmen had 
agrecd to the terms o : settlement Ex, M - 5 it must follor 
that the terms must be held to be fair and just. At this 
stage it will be pertinent for me to point out that the terms 
worked out in the settlement Ex, M - 5 are in perfect con 
sonance with the guidelines laid down in the Award of Arbi 
tration Board for Cement Industry 1979 Ex . M - 6 , Thorefore 
in the larger interest of industrial peace and for the sake of 
uniformity in service conditions the terms of the Award of 
Arbitration Board for Cement Industry 1978 had to be imple 
mented . In 1973 – 11 – L . L .J – Page 144 ( The workmen of 
Government Silk Weaving Factory, Mysore Vs. The Presiding 
Officer, Industrial Tribunal, Bangalore and others ) , the 
Supreme Court held that where a Tribunal has rocorded a 
finding to the effect that the settlement was bewoon a sub 
stantial number of workmen on the one hand and the manage 
ment of the pending lisputes , that it was a genuine settle 
ment and the terms of the settlement were very fair and just. 
In the light of the aforesaid decision of the Supreme Court, 
the present settlement Ex . M - 5 based on the Award of Arbi 
tration Board for Cement Industry 1978 Ex. M -6 must bo 
held to be very fair and just. In 1979 (54 ) F .J . R . - -Page 249 
(Herbertsons Limited vs . their workmen and others ) the 
Supreme Court has laid down guidelines as to when a given 
settlement can be held to be just and fair, The Supreme 
Court points out that as far as the parties are concerned there 
will always be uncertainty with regard to the ultimat . result 
of the litigation in Court proceedings . In the instant case , 
the litigation thus far had a checkered carcor. The reference 
to this Court was made by the Government as early on 27th 
February , 1976 . Thercafter for obtaining the Assessor s 
report, considered time has been spent. The Assessor s Report 
is marked as Ex. M - 1 . All the four Unions have fllod obicc 
tions to the Assessor s Report. Thereafter all the Unions and 
thç Management had examined witnesses on their behalf, At 
that juncture the Award of Arbitration Board for Cement 
Industry was rendered . W hereupon all the parties including 
the Management and the four Unions had agreed to work 
out the remedies pursuant to the Award of Arbitration Board 
Cur Cement Industry 1978 Ex. M -6 . The four Uniong started 
discussions with the Management for about 3 or 4 months. 
Eventually , three Unions representing the majority of work 
men and agreed to the term of Ex, M - 7 which were worked 
out in the Settlement Ex. M - 5 . Now two Unions would like 
to dis- associate themselves from the settlement. Already liti 
gations were pending for about 4 years and there will be no 
knowing as to what would be the ultimate fate of the dispute . 
It should also be remembered that the Respondent 
Management is not a big concern but is only 
employed about 173 workmen in the limestono mines . Taking 
into consideration tho job evaluation report Ex. M - 1 and the 
Award of Arbitration Board for Cement Industry . Ex . M -6 
and the never ending litigations, it is not improbablo that on 
a mature consideration and calm reflection , the workmen had 
decided to como to terme under Ex . M - 5 . As the Supremo 
Court has poipted out that it is not possible to scan a settle 
ment in bite and pieces and hold sono parts good and accept 
ablc and others had . It should also be borne in mind that 
there is absolutely no allegation of mala fides or fraud or 
even corruption or other inducements for the workmen to 
have agreed to terms of settlement Ex . M - 5 . The terms of 
settlement must be held to be gepuine. On an over all cxa 
mination of the materials oral, documeutary and the Assessor s 
Report Ex . M - 1 and llie Award of Arbitration Board for 
Cement Industry Ex . M - 6 , it must be held that all the cate 
gories of workmen working under the Respondent -Manago 
ment had been given proper nomenclture and proper fitment 
in the light of the Award under Ex. M - 6 . No doubt it is 
pointed out by Union No. 4 Cement Workers Progressivo 
Union that some of the torms under Ex. M - 5 are cot very 
sutisfactory . The terms may not be satisfactory As far as 
the Union is concerned . But there are several factors influz 
encing the parties to come to a gettloment in the course of 
collective bargaining. Ex . M - 3 19 a Comparative Statement 
of various grades and designations prevailing in quarties 
attached to cemont factories in Tamil Nadu ag on date in 
Auguat, 1978 . Those grades and designations obtained in 
those quarties very favourably compared with the grades and 
designations now Anally settled under Ex, M - 5. Further 
more , the job evalution report Ex. M - 1 which is done by an 
independent body, viz ., Madras Productivity Council would 
spoak volimog of tho justness of the grades and designations 
given to the workmen of the Respondent Corporation undor 
tho settlement Ex. M - 5. The designation and grades and 


( 5 ) It remains to be considered whether there is any sub 
stance in the objection filed by Union No. 1 , viz ., Chettinad 
Cement Corporation Uzhaippalar Sangam . This objection 
has bçen marked as Ex . W -11. One and the only objection 
running through this is that the settlement will not be in 
thro interest of the majority of the employees . I had earlier 
referred to this respect of affecting majority and minority 
workers . The interest of the employec remains the gamo 
bo it in a majority or a minority . Admittedly this Union 
has no substantial following. It is not their claim either . 
Their contention appears to be that the terms of settlement 
must be ignored and the dispute must be determined by this 
Tribunal itself on the basis of full and fresh enquiry . I had 
earlier pointed out how all the four Unions Including this 
Union had agreed to abide by the Award of Arbitration 
Board for Cement Industry 1978 whose report is marked as 
Ex, M - 6 . Therefore having consented to abide by this Award 
it does not lie in the mouth of this Union at this stage to 
contend that the issues at stake in the present dispute must. 
be decided by this Tribunal on the basis of a full and frosb 
enquiry . At page 78 paragraph 207 of Ex. M - 6 point out 
that " where the reference to such Labour Court or Industrial 
Court or Industrial Tribunal had been made earlier to the 
date of reference for arbitration before us we would suggest 
to the parties that in the larger interests of Industrial peace 
in the country and for the sake of uniformity of service condi 
tions as far as possible , the parties may approach th , Lahour 
Court or the Industrial Tribunal or the Industrial Court 
concerned with a joint request to pass an award in terms of 
our award ." The reference to the Arbitration Board for 
Cement Industry was inadt on 24 - 12 - 1977 whereas the pre 
sent refcronce to this Tilbunal has been made by the Govern

Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947 ), the Central Goverament 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern 
ment Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta in the jindustrial dispute 
between the cmployer in relation to the management of 
Bhanora Colliery of Bhanora Sub -Area of Eastern Coalfields 
Limited , P . O . Charanpur , Distt. Burdwan and their work 
men , which was received by the Central Government On

5. The management in their pleadings have taken the point 
that the reference is not competent because no Jispute was 
ever luised by or on behalf of the concerned workman at any 
[ imc with the employers. Only a demand was pumpurted to 
have becn addressed by the Colliery Mazdoor Congress to the 
Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central), Asangol, a copy 
of which was received hy the management. This night have 
been at best a demand but it cannot be the basis of the valid 
industrial dispute .

6 . It is then submitted that the demand addressed to the 
Assistant Labour Commissioner was on bebralf of the Gene 
ral Secretary , Colliery Mazdoor Congress, That union 
did not pursue the subsequent proceedings before the Aspis 
tant Labour Commissioner . On the contrary , Sri J . Pandey, 
claiming himself to be the Secretary of the Union kpown as 
Colllery Mazdoor Congress ( Ind . ) took up the matter . The 
concerned Union, therefore , had no jurisdiction or focus 
standi to sponsor the dispute ,

of the management it is stated that the management of Bhanora 
colliery was taken over by the Govt . of Todia under the Coal

large number of casual wagon loaders used to be employed ut 
the colliery . After the take over , the old system continued for 
some time till there was a screening in 1973 . Eventually a 
list of casual wagon loaders was prepared and wagon load 
ing is done at present by deploying these workers an ad 
when required .

By Order No . L - 19012 / 17 / 76 - D . III B dated 26th Novom 
ber , 1976 , the Government of India , Ministry of Labour, 
referred an industrial dispute existing between the manage 
inent of Bhanora Colliery and Shri Samrathi Yadav, to this 
Tribunal, for adjudication . The schedule to the order of re 
ference reads as : 


" Whether the management of Dhanora Colliery of Bha 

nora Sub -Arca of Eastern Coalficlds Lid ., P . O . 
Charanpur , Dist, Burdwan was justified in denying 
employment to Shri Samrathi Yaday with effect from 
4 - 10 - 1974 ? If not, what relief the workman isen 
titled to 7" 


9 . From January 1974 declaration forms were obtained from 
casual wagon loaders to enable the employer to preparo 
pool of casual wagon londers . On 3rd August, 1974 one porn 
son said to be Samrathi Yadav, son of Yohar Yadav got him 
self enroled after filling up the declaration form . Soon thero 
after he was detected to be not the concerned Samrathi 
Yadav who had worked carlier and soon after the detection 
Was macle he was stopped from working from 4th Oct. 1974 , 
As it was a clear case of in personation the employer had to 
stop him from working in the colliery . Moreover , a carval 
workman whose service , begin in the morning and ends in 
the evening, if at all work can be provided for him , had no 
right as such to work as a casual worker. There can not 
therefore be any valid rispute regarding employment or non 
employment of such m person whose very nature of work 
is transient in character and is otherwise dependant upon a 
number of other factors . 


2 . The case a9 pleaded on behalf of Samrathi Yadav , tho 
concerned workman , is that he was employed by Equitable 
Coal Company Ltd ., che erstwhile owners of Bhanura col. 
liery as a wagon loading mazdoor. He had been working 
for about 3 or 4 years when the management of the colliery 
was taken over by the Central Government. On nationalisa 
tion of the colliery , he was employed as a wagon loader 
under Coal Mincs Authority Lad. And thercafter under Eas 
tern Coalfields Limited . 


10 . In answer to " he written statement fled on behalf 
of Sri Yadav , the management stated that the job of wagon 
loading may be a perenninl job but the deployment of wagon 
loaders depends entirely upon supply of wagons which is 
erratic , and for days there may not be any placement at all 
when necessarily such casual wagon londers cannot be em 
ployed . The management denied shat the concerned work 
man was working for more than 24 days in a month , Holl 
not entitled to any relief . 


3 . In paragraph 2 of the writton statement Aled on behalt 
of the concerned workman it is stated that though the work 
was of permanent nature and he had been working for more 
tban 24 days in a month , continually and regularly , the pri 
vate management in order to deprive him and others of their 
legitimate dues did not make them permanent and the con 
cerned workman was designated as casual wagun loader 
though he performed the job of a permanent nature . 


11 . At the hearing of the roterenco , the learned advocate 
did not press the objection as to the maintainability of the 
reference on the grolind that no demand had bcon made 
and the Union had no locir standi to espouse the cause of 
the concerned workman . 


4 . The concerned workman was given bonus card and his 
name was also entered in the B form register. All of a sud 
den , he was stopped from working with effect from 4th Octo 
ber, 1974 on the ground that he was got the real Sanirathi 
Yadav . The denial of employment to the concerned work 
man by the managemert is illogal and he is entitled to be 
re - employed with effect from 4th October, 1974 with all 
back wages and other benefits which he should have earned

a person working in the colliery in 1970 . There were as 
many as seven of eight persons named Samrathi but there was 
no other man of the name of Samrathi Yadav . Samrath
